to stay close to shardUpdateDuration.\n1112: \t\t\tselect {\n1113: \t\t\tcase t.reshardChan <- desiredShards:\n1114: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Remote storage resharding\", \"from\", t.numShards, \"to\", desiredShards)\n1115: \t\t\t\tt.numShards = desiredShards\n1116: \t\t\tdefault:\n1117: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Currently resharding, skipping.\")\n1118: \t\t\t}\n1119: \t\tcase <-t.quit:\n1120: desiredShards == t.numShards {\n1128: \t\treturn false\n1129: \t}\n1130: \t// We shouldn't reshard if Prometheus hasn't been able to send\n1131: \t// since the last time it checked if it should reshard.\n1132: \tminSendTimestamp := time.Now().Add(-1 * shardUpdateDuration).Unix()\n1133: \tlsts := t.lastSendTimestamp.Load()\n1134: \tif lsts < minSendTimestamp {\n1135: \t\tt.logger.Warn(\"Skipping resharding, last successful send was beyond threshold\", \"lastSendTimestamp\", lsts, \"minSendTimestamp\", minSendTimestamp)\n1136: \t\treturn false\n1137: \t}\n1138: It is up to the caller to reshard, or not,\n1151: // based on the return value.\n1152: func (t *QueueManager) calculateDesiredShards() int {\n1153: \tt.dataOut.tick()\n1154: \tt.dataDropped.tick()\n1155: \tt.dataOutDuration.tick()\n1156: \n1157: \t// We use the number of incoming samples as a prediction of how much work we\n1158: \t// will need to do next iteration.  We add to this any pending samples\n1159: \t// (received - send) so we can catch up with any backlog. We use the average\n1160: \t// outgoing batch latency to work out how many shards we need.\n1161: \tvar (\n1162: \t\tdataInRate      = t.dataIn.rate()\n1163: \t\tdataOutRate     = t.dataOut.rate()\n1164: \t\tdataKeptRatio   = dataOutRate / (t.dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)\n1165: \t\tdataOutDuration = t.dataOutDuration.rate() / float64(time.Second)\n1166: \t\tdataPendingRate = dataInRate*dataKeptRatio - shardToleranceFraction)\n1201: \t\tupperBound = float64(t.numShards) * (1. + shardToleranceFraction)\n1202: \t)\n1203: \tt.logger.Debug(\"QueueManager.updateShardsLoop\",\n1204: \t\t\"lowerBound\", lowerBound, \"desiredShards\", desiredShards, \"upperBound\", upperBound)\n1205: \n1206: \tdesiredShards = math.Ceil(desiredShards) // Round up to be on the safe side.\n1207: \tif lowerBound <= desiredShards && desiredShards <= upperBound {\n1208: \t\treturn t.numShards\n1209: \t}\n1210: \n1211: \tnumShards := int(desiredShards)\n1212: \t// Do not downshard if we are more than ten seconds back.\n1213: \tif numShards < t.numShards && delay > 10.0 {\n1214: \t\tt.logger.Debug(\"Not downsharding due to being too far behind\")\n1215: \t\treturn t.numShards\n1216: \t}\n1217: \n1218: \tswitch {\n1219: \tcase numShards > t.cfg.MaxShards:\n1220: \t\tnumShards = t.cfg.MaxShards\n1221: \tcase numShards < t.cfg.MinShards:\n1222: \t\tnumShards = t.cfg.MinShards\n1223: \t}\n1224: \treturn numShards\n1225: }\n1226: \n1227: func (t *QueueManager) reshardLoop() {\n1228: \tdefer t.wg.Done()\n1229: \n1230: \tfor {\n1231: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/prometheus/prometheus@3155c95c1f58542ed98d265b0bae6675df433f8a/-/blob/storage/remote/queue_manager.go%3FL1050-1230"}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01MwnSJXj6Kyzay9JswLqCvk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Tr4PJtTCKbypqduZn4sGhK","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/investigation.md","content":"# Investigation Report\n\n## Summary\n\nRemote-write queue resharding stalls because commit `184c7eb` moved `dataIn` tracking from the `WriteStorage`-level `timestampTracker.Commit()` (which runs unconditionally at scrape commit time) into `shards.enqueue()` (which blocks when queues are full). This causes the measured incoming data rate (`dataIn`) to be artificially capped at the outgoing rate (`dataOut`), preventing `calculateDesiredShards()` from ever computing a higher shard count. The same commit also removed the `dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio` from the shard calculation, and replaced the `WriteStorage`-shared `highestRecvTimestamp` with a per-queue `highestTimestamp` metric updated inside `enqueue()`, compounding the problem.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n**File:** `storage/remote/queue_manager.go`\n**Functions:** `shards.enqueue()` (line 1312), `calculateDesiredShards()` (line 1111)\n**Commit:** `184c7eb9` (\"storage/remote: compute highestTimestamp and dataIn at QueueManager level\")\n\n### Mechanism\n\nPrior to the change, the data flow for shard calculation worked as follows:\n\n1. **`dataIn` tracked at `WriteStorage` level:** `timestampTracker.Commit()` in `write.go` called `rws.samplesIn.incr()` every scrape cycle, unconditionally. This `samplesIn` EWMA was shared across all `QueueManager` instances and ticked by a dedicated `WriteStorage.run()` goroutine.\n\n2. **`highestRecvTimestamp` tracked at `WriteStorage` level:** Set in `timestampTracker.Commit()` to the highest timestamp from the scrape, regardless of whether samples were enqueued.\n\n3. **`dataDropped` tracked in `Append()`:** When a series was filtered by relabeling, `dataDropped.incr(1)` was called, and `calculateDesiredShards()` used a `dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)` to adjust the incoming rate.\n\nThe commit `184c7eb` changed this to:\n\n1. **`dataIn` moved into `shards.enqueue()` (line 1338):** `s.qm.dataIn.incr(1)` is called only when a sample is **successfully** enqueued into a shard queue.\n\n2. **`highestTimestamp` moved into `shards.enqueue()` (line 1337):** `s.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set()` is called only upon successful enqueue.\n\n3. **`dataDropped` removed entirely:** The `dataKeptRatio` calculation was eliminated from `calculateDesiredShards()`.\n\n4. **`dataIn.tick()` moved into `calculateDesiredShards()` (line 1112):** Instead of being ticked by an independent goroutine in `WriteStorage.run()`, it's now ticked only when the shard calculation runs.\n\n### The Deadlock/Stall\n\nThe critical problem is in step 1. When the `Append()` method (line 726) tries to enqueue a sample, the flow is:\n\n```\nAppend() -> shards.enqueue() -> queues[shard].Append() -> if full, return false\n```\n\nWhen `enqueue()` returns false (queue full), `Append()` enters a backoff-retry loop (lines 756-780). During this blocking period:\n\n- **`dataIn` is NOT incremented** because `enqueue()` only calls `dataIn.incr(1)` on successful enqueue (line 1338).\n- The rate at which `dataIn` is incremented is therefore **bounded by the rate at which shards drain their queues** (i.e., `dataOut`).\n- When `calculateDesiredShards()` runs, it sees `dataInRate ~= dataOutRate`, computes `dataPendingRate ~= 0`, and `desiredShards ~= current shards`.\n- The `highestTimestamp` metric also stalls because it's only updated on successful enqueue, so `delay` (line 1127) also underestimates the backlog.\n\nThis creates a **feedback loop**: queues are full -> dataIn can't be measured accurately -> resharding doesn't trigger -> queues stay full.\n\n### Why It's Intermittent\n\nThe issue manifests when:\n\n1. **Target discovery changes** cause a burst of new series, increasing the scrape rate beyond current shard capacity.\n2. **Queue capacity fills up** (controlled by `queue_config.capacity`, default 10000 with `max_samples_per_send` of 500 = 20 batch slots).\n3. The timing depends on the rate of incoming samples vs. current shard throughput. If samples arrive slowly enough that queues never fill completely, resharding works correctly because `enqueue()` succeeds and `dataIn` reflects the true rate.\n4. The stall is more likely with **relabeled endpoints** that only accept a subset of series, because the old `dataKeptRatio` compensation was also removed.\n\nThe 30% tolerance band (`shardToleranceFraction = 0.3`, line 61) can mask small discrepancies, but once queues are saturated, the measured `dataInRate` becomes exactly `dataOutRate`, and `desiredShards` stays within the tolerance band of `numShards`.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Code References (local working tree matches Sourcegraph HEAD)\n\n- **`shards.enqueue()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1312-1339`: `dataIn.incr(1)` at line 1338 only executes on successful enqueue. If `queues[shard].Append(data)` returns false (line 1321), the function returns false without incrementing `dataIn`.\n\n- **`QueueManager.Append()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:726-782`: Retry loop at line 756 blocks with exponential backoff when `enqueue()` returns false. During blocking, no `dataIn` increments occur.\n\n- **`calculateDesiredShards()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1111-1182`: Uses `dataInRate` (line 1121) which is now only incremented inside `enqueue()`. The formula `desiredShards = timePerSample * (dataInRate + backlogCatchup)` at line 1140 will underestimate when `dataInRate` is capped.\n\n- **`highestTimestamp` tracking** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1337`: `s.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set(float64(data.timestamp / 1000))` only fires on successful enqueue, so the `delay` calculation at line 1127 (`highestRecv - highestSent`) also underestimates the true backlog.\n\n- **`updateShardsLoop()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1098-1123`: Runs every `shardUpdateDuration` (10s). Calls `calculateDesiredShards()` and `shouldReshard()`. When the calculated desired shards equals current shards, resharding is skipped.\n\n- **`reshardLoop()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1227-1242`: Receives on `reshardChan`, calls `shards.stop()` then `shards.start(numShards)`. During `stop()`, `softShutdown` is closed (line 1275) causing `enqueue()` to return false, which triggers `Append()` retry loops.\n\n- **Removed `dataDropped`** - The `dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio` were removed in commit `184c7eb`. Previously `calculateDesiredShards()` at line 1123 used `dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (t.dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)` to account for relabel-filtered series. Without this, `dataInRate` (which now only counts enqueued samples) is compared directly to `dataOutRate`.\n\n- **Removed shared `samplesIn`** - `storage/remote/write.go:60-78`: The `WriteStorage` struct no longer has a `samplesIn` field or a `run()` goroutine to tick it. The `timestampTracker.Commit()` at line 331 no longer increments any per-QueueManager EWMA.\n\n### Commit History\n\n| Commit | Description | Impact |\n|--------|-------------|--------|\n| `184c7eb` | \"storage/remote: compute highestTimestamp and dataIn at QueueManager level\" | **Root cause**: Moved `dataIn` and `highestTimestamp` into `enqueue()`, removed `dataDropped`, removed shared `samplesIn` EWMA |\n| `ba14bc49` | \"chore: deprecate prometheus_remote_storage_{samples,exemplars,histograms}_in_total...\" | Deprecated old WriteStorage-level metrics that were the previous source of truth |\n| `6806b68` (release-3.7 only) | \"fix: Remote-write: revert changes in the queue resharding logic\" | Reverts both commits above on the release branch, restoring correct behavior |\n\n### GitHub Issue\n\n- **Issue [#17384](https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/issues/17384)**: Reports instances lagging >1 hour after upgrade, shard count stuck at 3-4 despite low resource utilization. Confirmed as caused by `dataIn` being capped at `dataOut` rate.\n\n- **PR [#17412](https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/pull/17412)**: Reverts the problematic changes on the `release-3.7` branch.\n\n## Affected Components\n\n| Package/Module | File | Impact |\n|---------------|------|--------|\n| `storage/remote` | `queue_manager.go` | Core resharding logic: `calculateDesiredShards()`, `shards.enqueue()`, `updateShardsLoop()` |\n| `storage/remote` | `write.go` | `WriteStorage` no longer provides shared `samplesIn` EWMA or runs tick goroutine; `timestampTracker.Commit()` no longer feeds QueueManager dataIn |\n| `storage/remote` | `ewma.go` | EWMA rate tracking (`ewmaRate.incr()` / `.tick()` / `.rate()`) — behavior unchanged but inputs are now wrong |\n| Dashboards/Alerts | `documentation/prometheus-mixin/` | Alert `PrometheusRemoteWriteBehind` was updated to use new `queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` which also stalls |\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Fix Strategy\n\nThe fix (already applied on `release-3.7` via commit `6806b68`) is to revert commits `184c7eb` and `ba14bc49` on `main`. This restores:\n\n1. **`dataIn` tracking at `WriteStorage.timestampTracker.Commit()`** — incremented unconditionally on every scrape commit, giving an accurate incoming sample rate independent of queue state.\n2. **Shared `samplesIn` EWMA with dedicated tick goroutine** — ensuring the rate is computed on a regular interval regardless of queue saturation.\n3. **`dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio`** — accounting for relabel-filtered series in the shard calculation.\n4. **`highestRecvTimestamp` at `WriteStorage` level** — providing accurate backlog delay measurement.\n\nIf the per-queue metrics goal from PR #17065 is to be preserved, the correct approach would be to track `dataIn` at a point **before** the blocking enqueue — for example, incrementing `dataIn` inside `Append()` before the retry loop, or maintaining the `WriteStorage`-level EWMA while also providing per-queue metrics for observability.\n\n### Diagnostic Steps to Confirm\n\n1. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` gauge**: If stuck at or near `prometheus_remote_storage_shards` despite growing backlog, the shard calculation is being starved of accurate input rate data.\n\n2. **Compare `prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` vs `prometheus_remote_storage_highest_timestamp_in_seconds`**: If the queue-level metric lags significantly behind the storage-level metric, it confirms that `highestTimestamp` updates are blocked by full queues.\n\n3. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_enqueue_retries_total`**: A rapidly increasing counter confirms that `Append()` is frequently blocked in its retry loop, meaning `enqueue()` is returning false and `dataIn` is not being incremented.\n\n4. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_pending`**: If stuck at a high value with no progress (not increasing or decreasing), shards are saturated but no resharding is occurring.\n\n5. **Enable debug logging** and look for `QueueManager.calculateDesiredShards` log lines: `dataInRate` approximately equal to `dataOutRate` with `dataPending` near zero despite known backlog confirms the feedback loop.\n\n6. **Monitor `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_dropped_total` with reason `unintentionally_dropped_series`**: High values indicate samples are being lost during the stall.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":122,"cache_read_input_tokens":100106,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":122,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"59f36f17-59e6-497e-b488-e5de2750a07a","uuid":"bb5c810a-7f93-4e64-a206-96cea81c6ff4"}

This causes the measured incoming data rate (`dataIn`) to be artificially capped at the outgoing rate (`dataOut`), preventing `calculateDesiredShards()` from ever computing a higher shard count. The same commit also removed the `dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio` from the shard calculation, and replaced the `WriteStorage`-shared `highestRecvTimestamp` with a per-queue `highestTimestamp` metric updated inside `enqueue()`, compounding the problem.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n**File:** `storage/remote/queue_manager.go`\n**Functions:** `shards.enqueue()` (line 1312), `calculateDesiredShards()` (line 1111)\n**Commit:** `184c7eb9` (\"storage/remote: compute highestTimestamp and dataIn at QueueManager level\")\n\n### Mechanism\n\nPrior to the change, the data flow for shard calculation worked as follows:\n\n1. **`dataIn` tracked at `WriteStorage` level:** `timestampTracker.Commit()` in `write.go` called `rws.samplesIn.incr()` every scrape cycle, unconditionally. This `samplesIn` EWMA was shared across all `QueueManager` instances and ticked by a dedicated `WriteStorage.run()` goroutine.\n\n2. **`highestRecvTimestamp` tracked at `WriteStorage` level:** Set in `timestampTracker.Commit()` to the highest timestamp from the scrape, regardless of whether samples were enqueued.\n\n3. **`dataDropped` tracked in `Append()`:** When a series was filtered by relabeling, `dataDropped.incr(1)` was called, and `calculateDesiredShards()` used a `dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)` to adjust the incoming rate.\n\nThe commit `184c7eb` changed this to:\n\n1. **`dataIn` moved into `shards.enqueue()` (line 1338):** `s.qm.dataIn.incr(1)` is called only when a sample is **successfully** enqueued into a shard queue.\n\n2. **`highestTimestamp` moved into `shards.enqueue()` (line 1337):** `s.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set()` is called only upon successful enqueue.\n\n3. **`dataDropped` removed entirely:** The `dataKeptRatio` calculation was eliminated from `calculateDesiredShards()`.\n\n4. **`dataIn.tick()` moved into `calculateDesiredShards()` (line 1112):** Instead of being ticked by an independent goroutine in `WriteStorage.run()`, it's now ticked only when the shard calculation runs.\n\n### The Deadlock/Stall\n\nThe critical problem is in step 1. When the `Append()` method (line 726) tries to enqueue a sample, the flow is:\n\n```\nAppend() -> shards.enqueue() -> queues[shard].Append() -> if full, return false\n```\n\nWhen `enqueue()` returns false (queue full), `Append()` enters a backoff-retry loop (lines 756-780). During this blocking period:\n\n- **`dataIn` is NOT incremented** because `enqueue()` only calls `dataIn.incr(1)` on successful enqueue (line 1338).\n- The rate at which `dataIn` is incremented is therefore **bounded by the rate at which shards drain their queues** (i.e., `dataOut`).\n- When `calculateDesiredShards()` runs, it sees `dataInRate ~= dataOutRate`, computes `dataPendingRate ~= 0`, and `desiredShards ~= current shards`.\n- The `highestTimestamp` metric also stalls because it's only updated on successful enqueue, so `delay` (line 1127) also underestimates the backlog.\n\nThis creates a **feedback loop**: queues are full -> dataIn can't be measured accurately -> resharding doesn't trigger -> queues stay full.\n\n### Why It's Intermittent\n\nThe issue manifests when:\n\n1. **Target discovery changes** cause a burst of new series, increasing the scrape rate beyond current shard capacity.\n2. **Queue capacity fills up** (controlled by `queue_config.capacity`, default 10000 with `max_samples_per_send` of 500 = 20 batch slots).\n3. The timing depends on the rate of incoming samples vs. current shard throughput. If samples arrive slowly enough that queues never fill completely, resharding works correctly because `enqueue()` succeeds and `dataIn` reflects the true rate.\n4. The stall is more likely with **relabeled endpoints** that only accept a subset of series, because the old `dataKeptRatio` compensation was also removed.\n\nThe 30% tolerance band (`shardToleranceFraction = 0.3`, line 61) can mask small discrepancies, but once queues are saturated, the measured `dataInRate` becomes exactly `dataOutRate`, and `desiredShards` stays within the tolerance band of `numShards`.\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Code References (local working tree matches Sourcegraph HEAD)\n\n- **`shards.enqueue()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1312-1339`: `dataIn.incr(1)` at line 1338 only executes on successful enqueue. If `queues[shard].Append(data)` returns false (line 1321), the function returns false without incrementing `dataIn`.\n\n- **`QueueManager.Append()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:726-782`: Retry loop at line 756 blocks with exponential backoff when `enqueue()` returns false. During blocking, no `dataIn` increments occur.\n\n- **`calculateDesiredShards()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1111-1182`: Uses `dataInRate` (line 1121) which is now only incremented inside `enqueue()`. The formula `desiredShards = timePerSample * (dataInRate + backlogCatchup)` at line 1140 will underestimate when `dataInRate` is capped.\n\n- **`highestTimestamp` tracking** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1337`: `s.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set(float64(data.timestamp / 1000))` only fires on successful enqueue, so the `delay` calculation at line 1127 (`highestRecv - highestSent`) also underestimates the true backlog.\n\n- **`updateShardsLoop()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1098-1123`: Runs every `shardUpdateDuration` (10s). Calls `calculateDesiredShards()` and `shouldReshard()`. When the calculated desired shards equals current shards, resharding is skipped.\n\n- **`reshardLoop()`** - `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1227-1242`: Receives on `reshardChan`, calls `shards.stop()` then `shards.start(numShards)`. During `stop()`, `softShutdown` is closed (line 1275) causing `enqueue()` to return false, which triggers `Append()` retry loops.\n\n- **Removed `dataDropped`** - The `dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio` were removed in commit `184c7eb`. Previously `calculateDesiredShards()` at line 1123 used `dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (t.dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)` to account for relabel-filtered series. Without this, `dataInRate` (which now only counts enqueued samples) is compared directly to `dataOutRate`.\n\n- **Removed shared `samplesIn`** - `storage/remote/write.go:60-78`: The `WriteStorage` struct no longer has a `samplesIn` field or a `run()` goroutine to tick it. The `timestampTracker.Commit()` at line 331 no longer increments any per-QueueManager EWMA.\n\n### Commit History\n\n| Commit | Description | Impact |\n|--------|-------------|--------|\n| `184c7eb` | \"storage/remote: compute highestTimestamp and dataIn at QueueManager level\" | **Root cause**: Moved `dataIn` and `highestTimestamp` into `enqueue()`, removed `dataDropped`, removed shared `samplesIn` EWMA |\n| `ba14bc49` | \"chore: deprecate prometheus_remote_storage_{samples,exemplars,histograms}_in_total...\" | Deprecated old WriteStorage-level metrics that were the previous source of truth |\n| `6806b68` (release-3.7 only) | \"fix: Remote-write: revert changes in the queue resharding logic\" | Reverts both commits above on the release branch, restoring correct behavior |\n\n### GitHub Issue\n\n- **Issue [#17384](https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/issues/17384)**: Reports instances lagging >1 hour after upgrade, shard count stuck at 3-4 despite low resource utilization. Confirmed as caused by `dataIn` being capped at `dataOut` rate.\n\n- **PR [#17412](https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/pull/17412)**: Reverts the problematic changes on the `release-3.7` branch.\n\n## Affected Components\n\n| Package/Module | File | Impact |\n|---------------|------|--------|\n| `storage/remote` | `queue_manager.go` | Core resharding logic: `calculateDesiredShards()`, `shards.enqueue()`, `updateShardsLoop()` |\n| `storage/remote` | `write.go` | `WriteStorage` no longer provides shared `samplesIn` EWMA or runs tick goroutine; `timestampTracker.Commit()` no longer feeds QueueManager dataIn |\n| `storage/remote` | `ewma.go` | EWMA rate tracking (`ewmaRate.incr()` / `.tick()` / `.rate()`) — behavior unchanged but inputs are now wrong |\n| Dashboards/Alerts | `documentation/prometheus-mixin/` | Alert `PrometheusRemoteWriteBehind` was updated to use new `queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` which also stalls |\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Fix Strategy\n\nThe fix (already applied on `release-3.7` via commit `6806b68`) is to revert commits `184c7eb` and `ba14bc49` on `main`. This restores:\n\n1. **`dataIn` tracking at `WriteStorage.timestampTracker.Commit()`** — incremented unconditionally on every scrape commit, giving an accurate incoming sample rate independent of queue state.\n2. **Shared `samplesIn` EWMA with dedicated tick goroutine** — ensuring the rate is computed on a regular interval regardless of queue saturation.\n3. **`dataDropped` EWMA and `dataKeptRatio`** — accounting for relabel-filtered series in the shard calculation.\n4. **`highestRecvTimestamp` at `WriteStorage` level** — providing accurate backlog delay measurement.\n\nIf the per-queue metrics goal from PR #17065 is to be preserved, the correct approach would be to track `dataIn` at a point **before** the blocking enqueue — for example, incrementing `dataIn` inside `Append()` before the retry loop, or maintaining the `WriteStorage`-level EWMA while also providing per-queue metrics for observability.\n\n### Diagnostic Steps to Confirm\n\n1. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` gauge**: If stuck at or near `prometheus_remote_storage_shards` despite growing backlog, the shard calculation is being starved of accurate input rate data.\n\n2. **Compare `prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` vs `prometheus_remote_storage_highest_timestamp_in_seconds`**: If the queue-level metric lags significantly behind the storage-level metric, it confirms that `highestTimestamp` updates are blocked by full queues.\n\n3. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_enqueue_retries_total`**: A rapidly increasing counter confirms that `Append()` is frequently blocked in its retry loop, meaning `enqueue()` is returning false and `dataIn` is not being incremented.\n\n4. **Check `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_pending`**: If stuck at a high value with no progress (not increasing or decreasing), shards are saturated but no resharding is occurring.\n\n5. **Enable debug logging** and look for `QueueManager.calculateDesiredShards` log lines: `dataInRate` approximately equal to `dataOutRate` with `dataPending` near zero despite known backlog confirms the feedback loop.\n\n6. **Monitor `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_dropped_total` with reason `unintentionally_dropped_series`**: High values indicate samples are being lost during the stall.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
